height=0.4pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-0.2pt}\kern-0.2pt\crcr}}$}}}\,,}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}

and we obtain the summary

\phantom{M}SS

\phantom{M}TT

\phantom{M}UU

\phantom{M}VV

row-closed

\checkmark

\checkmark

\checkmark

left row-strict

\checkmark

\checkmark

\checkmark

cylindrical

\checkmark

\checkmark

.

Using Lindstrom’s Lemma
[
38
]
,
[
46
]
,
its permanental analogs
[
58
, Thm. 4.15]
,
and its power sum immanant analogs
[
58
, Thm. 4.16]

we may now extend Proposition
6.2

to include more combinatorial interpretations.

Proposition 9.1
.

Fix
w\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
avoiding the patterns
3412
and
4231
, with
corresponding zig-zag network
F_{w}
having path matrix
A
.
Let
P=P(w)
and
G=\mathrm{inc}(P)
be the corresponding
unit interval order and incomparability graph.
We have

(9.6)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Imm}_{\epsilon^{n}}^{\smash{\mathfrak{S}_{n}}}(A)=\det(A)

\displaystyle=\#\{U\in\mathcal{U}(P,1^{n})\,|\,U\text{ column-strict }\},

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}1&\text{if $G$ is an independent set ($w=e$ and $P$ is a chain)},\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(9.7)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Imm}_{\eta^{n}}^{\smash{\mathfrak{S}_{n}}}(A)=\mathrm{per}(A)

\displaystyle=\#\{U\in\mathcal{U}(F_{w},n)\,|\,U\text{ left row-strict}\,\},

\displaystyle=\#\{U\in\mathcal{U}(P,n)\,|\,U\text{ row-semistrict }\},

\displaystyle=\#\{U\in\mathcal{U}(P,n)\,|\,U\text{ excedance-free }\},

\displaystyle=\#\text{ acyclic orientations of $G$. }

(9.8)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Imm}_{\psi^{n}}^{\smash{\mathfrak{S}_{n}}}(A)

\displaystyle=\#\{U\in\mathcal{U}(F_{w},n)\,|\,U\text{ cylindrical}\,\},

\displaystyle=\#\{U\in\mathcal{U}(P,n)\,|\,U\text{ cyclically row-semistrict }\},

\displaystyle=\#\{U\in\mathcal{U}(P,n)\,|\,U\text{ record-free, row-semistrict }\},

\displaystyle=n\cdot\#\{U\in\mathcal{U}(P,n)\,|\,U\text{ right-anchored, row-semistrict }\},

\displaystyle=\#\text{ acyclic orientations of $G$ having exactly one source. }

By (
6.6
),
Proposition
9.1

gives interpretations of

\epsilon^{\lambda}(\widetilde{C}_{w}(1))
,

\eta^{\lambda}(\widetilde{C}_{w}(1))
,

\psi^{\lambda}(\widetilde{C}_{w}(1))

in the special case that
\lambda=n
.
The identities
(
6.4
) – (
6.5
) then lead
to results for general
\lambda
.
(See
[
17
, Thm. 4.7]
,
[
58
, Thms. 30–31]
.)

Theorem 9.2
.

Fix
w\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
avoiding the patterns
3412
and
4231
with
corresponding zig-zag network
F_{w}

and unit interval order
P=P(w)
as in (
7.1
).
For each partition
\lambda\vdash n
we have the following.

(i
-
a)

\epsilon^{\lambda}(C^{\prime}_{w}(1))=\#\{U\in\mathcal{U}(P,\lambda^{\negthickspace\top\negthickspace}\,)\,|\,U\text{ column-strict}\,\}
.

(i
-
b)

\epsilon^{\lambda}(C^{\prime}_{w}(1))=\#\text{ colorings of $\mathrm{inc}(P)$ of type $\lambda$.}

(ii
-
a)

\eta^{\lambda}(C^{\prime}_{w}(1))=\#\{U\in\mathcal{U}(F_{w},\lambda)\,|\,U\text{ row-closed, left row-strict}\,\}
.

(ii
-
b)

\eta^{\lambda}(C^{\prime}_{w}(1))=\#\{U\in\mathcal{U}(P,\lambda)\,|\,U\text{ row-semistrict}\,\}
.

(ii
-
c)

\eta^{\lambda}(C^{\prime}_{w}(1))=\#\{U\in\mathcal{U}(P,\lambda)\,|\,U\text{ excedance-free}\,\}
.

(iii)

\chi^{\lambda}(C^{\prime}_{w}(1))=\#\{U\in\mathcal{U}(P,\lambda)\,|\,U\text{ standard}\,\}
.

(iv
-
a)

\psi^{\lambda}(C^{\prime}_{w}(1))=\#\{U\in\mathcal{U}(F_{w},\lambda)\,|\,U\text{ cylindrical}\,\,\}
.

(iv
-
b)

\psi^{\lambda}(C^{\prime}_{w}(1))=\#\{U\in\mathcal{U}(P,\lambda)\,|\,U\text{ cyclically row-semistrict}\,\}
.

(iv
-
c)

\psi^{\lambda}(C^{\prime}_{w}(1))=\#\{U\in\mathcal{U}(P,\lambda)\,|\,U\text{ record-free, row-semistrict}\,\}
.

(iv
-
d)

\psi^{\lambda}(C^{\prime}_{w}(1))=\lambda_{1}\cdots\lambda_{r}\cdot\#\{U\in\mathcal{U}(P,\lambda)\,|\,U\text{ right-anchored, row-semistrict}\,\}
.

See
[
58
, Thm. 31]
for a proof of statement (
ii
-
c
)
and its
q
-analog;
see
[
17
, Thm. 4.7]

for proofs of other statements
and
[
15
, Cor. 31]
,
[
17
, §5–9]

for proofs of their
q
-analogs.
We may also interpret
\eta_{q}^{\lambda}(C^{\prime}_{w}(1))

and
\psi_{q}^{\lambda}(C^{\prime}_{w}(1))
in terms of acyclic orientations of sequences of
subgraphs of
\mathrm{inc}(P(w))

[
58
, Thm. 10, Thm. 13]
.

Theorem 9.3
.

Fix
w\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
avoiding the patterns
3412
and
4231
,
and define
P=P(w)
as in Subsection
7.1
.
For all
\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\dotsc,\lambda_{r})\vdash n
we have

(1)

\eta^{\lambda}(C^{\prime}_{w}(1))
equals the number of acyclic orientations
of subgraph sequences

(9.9)

(\mathrm{inc}(P_{I_{1}}),\dotsc,\mathrm{inc}(P_{I_{r}})),

where
(I_{1},\dotsc,I_{r})
is an ordered set partition of
[n]

of type
\lambda
.

(2)

\psi^{\lambda}(C^{\prime}_{w}(1))
equals the number of acyclic orientations
of subgraph sequences (
9.9
) in which
each subgraph
\mathrm{inc}(P_{I_{j}})
is connected and its orientation has
a unique source.

9.2.
Type-
\mathsf{BC}
trace evaluations

It is possible to extend Proposition
6.5
to include
interpretations of the functions there in terms of path tableaux,
poset tableaux, and acyclic orientations,
just as Theorem
9.2
extends
Propositions
6.2
and
9.1
.
To do this, we define
\mathsf{BC}
-analogs of poset tableaux and path tableaux
(and use the marked acylic orientations defined at the end of
Subsection
8.2
).

Given a type-
\mathsf{BC}
unit interval order
Q
,
define a
marked
Q
-tableau

to be a Young diagram
filled with elements of
Q
, in which a (possibly empty)
subset of grounded elements of
Q
is marked with stars.
Define
\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{BC}}(Q,\lambda)
to be the set of
marked
Q
-tableaux of shape
\lambda
.
The seven properties of
P
-tableaux stated after (
9.2
)
carry over in a straightforward way to
Q
-tableaux.
For example, the type-
\mathsf{BC}
unit interval order
Q=Q(\overline{1}3\overline{2})

and a few
row-semistrict
marked
Q
-tableaux of shape
3
are

to0.4pt{\hrule depth=0.4pt,height=0.0pt\vss\hbox to0.4pt{\vrule width=0.4pt,height=0.4pt\hss\vrule width=0.4pt}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-0.2pt}\kern-0.2pt,\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$2^{\star}$\hss}\vss}\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\vbox to0.4pt{\hrule depth=0.4pt,height=0.0pt\vss\hbox to0.4pt{\vrule width=0.4pt,height=0.4pt\hss\vrule width=0.4pt}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-0.2pt}\kern-0.2pt,\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$1$\hss}\vss}\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\vbox to0.4pt{\hrule depth=0.4pt,height=0.0pt\vss\hbox to0.4pt{\vrule width=0.4pt,height=0.4pt\hss\vrule width=0.4pt}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-0.2pt}\kern-0.2pt\crcr}}$}}}\,,\quad{{\hbox{$\vbox{\halign{&\mkcell{#}\cr\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$3$\hss}\vss}\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\vbox to0.4pt{\hrule depth=0.4pt,height=0.0pt\vss\hbox to0.4pt{\vrule width=0.4pt,height=0.4pt\hss\vrule width=0.4pt}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-0.2pt}\kern-0.2pt,\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$2$\hss}\vss}\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\vbox to0.4pt{\hrule depth=0.4pt,height=0.0pt\vss\hbox to0.4pt{\vrule width=0.4pt,height=0.4pt\hss\vrule width=0.4pt}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-0.2pt}\kern-0.2pt,\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$1^{\star}$\hss}\vss}\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\vbox to0.4pt{\hrule depth=0.4pt,height=0.0pt\vss\hbox to0.4pt{\vrule width=0.4pt,height=0.4pt\hss\vrule width=0.4pt}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-0.2pt}\kern-0.2pt\crcr}}$}}}\,,\quad{{\hbox{$\vbox{\halign{&\mkcell{#}\cr\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$3$\hss}\vss}\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\vbox to0.4pt{\hrule depth=0.4pt,height=0.0pt\vss\hbox to0.4pt{\vrule width=0.4pt,height=0.4pt\hss\vrule width=0.4pt}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-0.2pt}\kern-0.2pt,\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$2^{\star}$\hss}\vss}\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\vbox to0.4pt{\hrule depth=0.4pt,height=0.0pt\vss\hbox to0.4pt{\vrule width=0.4pt,height=0.4pt\hss\vrule width=0.4pt}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-0.2pt}\kern-0.2pt,\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$1^{\star}$\hss}\vss}\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\vbox to0.4pt{\hrule depth=0.4pt,height=0.0pt\vss\hbox to0.4pt{\vrule width=0.4pt,height=0.4pt\hss\vrule width=0.4pt}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-0.2pt}\kern-0.2pt\crcr}}$}}}\,.\end{gathered}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}

Given
type-
\mathsf{BC}
zig-zag network

F_{w}\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{BC}}_{\mathrm{Z}}([\overline{n},n])

and a path family

\pi=(\pi_{\overline{n}},\dotsc,\pi_{\overline{1}},\pi_{1},\dotsc,\pi_{n})\in\Pi^{\mathsf{BC}}(F_{w}),

define an
F_{w}
-tableau
,
or more specifically a
\pi
-tableau
, to be a
Young diagram
filled
with paths
(\pi_{1},\dotsc,\pi_{n})
.
Define

\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{BC}}(F_{w},\lambda)
to be the set of
(unmarked)
F_{w}
-tableaux of shape
\lambda
.
Properties of such tableaux are simple extensions of
properties of type-
\mathsf{A}

path tableaux stated before (
9.3
),
with sink indices replaced by their absolute values.
For
U\in\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{BC}}(F_{w},\lambda)
,
call
U

(1)

row-closed
if

\{|\mathrm{snk}(U_{i,1})|,\dotsc,|\mathrm{snk}(U_{i,\lambda_{i}})|\}=\{\mathrm{src}(U_{i,1}),\dotsc,\mathrm{src}(U_{i,\lambda_{i}})\}
for all
i
,

(2)

left row-strict
if

\mathrm{src}(U_{i,1})<\cdots<\mathrm{src}(U_{i,\lambda_{i}})
for all
i
,

(3)

cylindrical
if
|\mathrm{snk}(U_{i,j})|=\mathrm{src}(U_{i,j+1})
for
j=1,\dotsc,\lambda_{i}-1
and

|\mathrm{snk}(U_{i,\lambda_{i}})|=\mathrm{src}(U_{i,1})
.

For example, consider

F_{\overline{1}3\overline{2}}
in (
9.14
)
and let
\pi=(\pi_{\overline{3}},\pi_{\overline{2}},\pi_{\overline{1}},\pi_{1},\pi_{2},\pi_{3})

be the fourth path family shown there.
Then
\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{BC}}(F_{w},3)
and
\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{BC}}(F_{w},21)
contain row-closed tableaux
such as

{{\hbox{$\vbox{\halign{&\mkcell{#}\cr\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\pi_{1}$\hss}\vss}\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\vbox to0.4pt{\hrule depth=0.4pt,height=0.0pt\vss\hbox to0.4pt{\vrule width=0.4pt,height=0.4pt\hss\vrule width=0.4pt}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-0.2pt}\kern-0.2pt,\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\pi_{2}$\hss}\vss}\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\vbox to0.4pt{\hrule depth=0.4pt,height=0.0pt\vss\hbox to0.4pt{\vrule width=0.4pt,height=0.4pt\hss\vrule width=0.4pt}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-0.2pt}\kern-0.2pt,\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\pi_{3}$\hss}\vss}\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\vbox to0.4pt{\hrule depth=0.4pt,height=0.0pt\vss\hbox to0.4pt{\vrule width=0.4pt,height=0.4pt\hss\vrule width=0.4pt}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-0.2pt}\kern-0.2pt\crcr}}$}}}\,,\quad{{\hbox{$\vbox{\halign{&\mkcell{#}\cr\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\pi_{1}$\hss}\vss}\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\vbox to0.4pt{\hrule depth=0.4pt,height=0.0pt\vss\hbox to0.4pt{\vrule width=0.4pt,height=0.4pt\hss\vrule width=0.4pt}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-0.2pt}\kern-0.2pt|\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\pi_{3}$\hss}\vss}\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\vbox to0.4pt{\hrule depth=0.4pt,height=0.0pt\vss\hbox to0.4pt{\vrule width=0.4pt,height=0.4pt\hss\vrule width=0.4pt}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-0.2pt}\kern-0.2pt,\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\pi_{2}$\hss}\vss}\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\vbox to0.4pt{\hrule depth=0.4pt,height=0.0pt\vss\hbox to0.4pt{\vrule width=0.4pt,height=0.4pt\hss\vrule width=0.4pt}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-0.2pt}\kern-0.2pt\crcr}}$}}}\,,}}}}}}

the first of which is left-row strict and the second of which is cylindrical.

Left row-strict
F_{w}
-tableaux of shape
n

correspond bijectively
to path families in
\Pi^{\mathsf{BC}}(F_{w})
:

(9.10)

{{\hbox{$\vbox{\halign{&\mkcell{#}\cr\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\pi_{1}$\hss}\vss}\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\vbox to0.4pt{\hrule depth=0.4pt,height=0.0pt\vss\hbox to0.4pt{\vrule width=0.4pt,height=0.4pt\hss\vrule width=0.4pt}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-0.2pt}\kern-0.2pt\crcr}}$}}}\,\cdots{{\hbox{$\vbox{\halign{&\mkcell{#}\cr\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\pi_{n}$\hss}\vss}\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\vbox to0.4pt{\hrule depth=0.4pt,height=0.0pt\vss\hbox to0.4pt{\vrule width=0.4pt,height=0.4pt\hss\vrule width=0.4pt}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-0.2pt}\kern-0.2pt\crcr}}$}}}\quad\leftrightarrow\quad(\pi_{\overline{n}},\dotsc,\pi_{\overline{1}},\pi_{1},\dotsc,\pi_{n}).}}

These tableaux and path families also correspond bijectively to
marked acyclic orientations of
\mathrm{inc}(Q(w))
and to
certain subsets of marked
Q(w)
-tableaux.
To describe these correspondences, we first
define an equivalence relation on
\Pi^{\mathsf{BC}}(F_{w})

by declaring

(9.11)

\pi\sim\tau\quad\text{ if }\quad\varphi(\mathrm{type}(\pi))=\varphi(\mathrm{type}(\tau)),

where
\varphi:\mathfrak{B}_{n}\rightarrow\mathfrak{S}_{n}

is the map defined in (
2.5
).
In terms of paths in the two families,
\pi\sim\tau
if

|\mathrm{snk}(\pi_{i})|=|\mathrm{snk}(\tau_{i})|
for
i=1,\dotsc,n
.

The cardinality of an equivalence class (
9.11
)
depends on the number of
positive sources of
F_{w}
from which there exists
a path to a negative sink.
Specifically, if the related type-
\mathsf{BC}
unit interval order
Q(w)
has
k

grounded elements, then
each equivalence class consists of
2^{k}
families,
with exactly one family
\tau
in each class satisfying

\ell_{t}(\mathrm{type}(\tau))=0
, i.e.,
\mathrm{snk}(\pi_{i})>0
for
i=1,\dotsc,n
.
Thus we have the bijection

(9.12)

\displaystyle\Pi^{\mathsf{BC}}(F_{w})

\displaystyle\rightarrow\{(\tau,K)\in\Pi^{\mathsf{BC}}(F_{w})\times 2^{[k]}\,|\,\ell_{t}(\mathrm{type}(\tau))=0\}

\displaystyle\pi

\displaystyle\mapsto(\tau,\{\mathrm{snk}(\pi_{\overline{n}}),\dotsc,\mathrm{snk}(\pi_{\overline{1}})\}\cap\mathbb{N}).

Since
the positively indexed paths
\tau_{1},\dotsc,\tau_{n}
of
\tau

cover the upper half of
F_{w}
, i.e.,
the planar network
F_{\varphi(w)}\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{A}}_{\mathrm{D}}([n])
,
we also have the bijection

(9.13)

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{BC}}(F_{w},n)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{A}}(F_{\varphi(w)},n)\times 2^{[k]}

\displaystyle{{\hbox{$\vbox{\halign{&\mkcell{#}\cr\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\pi_{\negthinspace u_{1}}$\hss}\vss}\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\vbox to0.4pt{\hrule depth=0.4pt,height=0.0pt\vss\hbox to0.4pt{\vrule width=0.4pt,height=0.4pt\hss\vrule width=0.4pt}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-0.2pt}\kern-0.2pt\crcr}}$}}}\cdots{{\hbox{$\vbox{\halign{&\mkcell{#}\cr\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\pi_{\negthinspace u_{n}}$\hss}\vss}\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\vbox to0.4pt{\hrule depth=0.4pt,height=0.0pt\vss\hbox to0.4pt{\vrule width=0.4pt,height=0.4pt\hss\vrule width=0.4pt}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-0.2pt}\kern-0.2pt\crcr}}$}}}}}

\displaystyle\mapsto\big(\;{{\hbox{$\vbox{\halign{&\mkcell{#}\cr\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\tau_{\negthinspace u_{1}}$\hss}\vss}\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\vbox to0.4pt{\hrule depth=0.4pt,height=0.0pt\vss\hbox to0.4pt{\vrule width=0.4pt,height=0.4pt\hss\vrule width=0.4pt}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-0.2pt}\kern-0.2pt\crcr}}$}}}\cdots{{\hbox{$\vbox{\halign{&\mkcell{#}\cr\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\tau_{\negthinspace u_{n}}$\hss}\vss}\vbox to0.0pt{\vss\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\vbox to0.4pt{\hrule depth=0.4pt,height=0.0pt\vss\hbox to0.4pt{\vrule width=0.4pt,height=0.4pt\hss\vrule width=0.4pt}\vss\hrule height=0.4pt,depth=0.0pt}\kern-0.2pt}\kern-0.2pt\crcr}}$}}},\{\mathrm{snk}(\pi_{\overline{n}}),\dotsc,\mathrm{snk}(\pi_{\overline{1}})\}\cap\mathbb{N}\big),}}

which preserves the row-closed, left row-strict, and cylindrical
properties of tableaux.
For example consider the network
F_{\overline{1}3\overline{2}}

and the unique path family
\tau\in\Pi^{\mathsf{BC}}(F_{\overline{1}3\overline{2}})
of type
132
.
Since the type-
\mathsf{BC}

unit interval order
Q(\overline{1}3\overline{2})
has
2
grounded elements,
the equivalence class of
\tau
consists of four
path families encoded by
(\tau,K)

for subsets
K\subseteq\{1,2\}
,

(9.14)

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\negthickspace.

We can now relate certain sets of

F_{w}
-tableaux and marked
Q(w)
-tableaux as follows.

Lemma 9.4
.

For
F_{w}\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{BC}}_{\mathrm{D}}([\overline{n},n])
, and corresponding

Q=Q(w)
,
we have bijections

(i)

\{U\in\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{BC}}(F_{w},n)\,|\,U\text{ left row-strict}\,\}\overset{1-1}{\longleftrightarrow}\{U\in\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{BC}}(Q,n)\,|\,U\text{ descent-free}\,\},

(ii)

\{U\in\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{BC}}(F_{w},n)\,|\,U\text{ cylindrical}\,\}\overset{1-1}{\longleftrightarrow}\{U\in\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{BC}}(Q,n)\,|\,U\text{ cyclically row-semistrict}\,\}
.

Proof.

Let
k
be the number of grounded elements of
Q(w)
.
By
(
9.13
),
tableaux
on the left-hand side
of (i)
correspond bijectively to pairs

\{(U,K)\in\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{A}}(F_{\varphi(w)},n)\times 2^{[k]}\,|\,U\text{ left row-strict}\},

and by (
9.7
) these
correspond bijectively to

\{(V,K)\in\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{A}}(Q(w),n)\times 2^{[k]}\,|\,V\text{ descent-free}\,\}.

Elements of this set correspond bijectively to tableaux
on the right-hand side of
(i)
:
simply modify
V
by
marking entries belonging to
K
.
Similarly,
tableaux in the first set
of (ii)
correspond bijectively to pairs

\{(U,K)\in\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{A}}(F_{\varphi(w)},n)\times 2^{[k]}\,|\,U\text{ cylindrical}\,\},

and by (
9.8
) these
correspond bijectively to

\{(V,K)\in\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{A}}(Q(w),n)\times 2^{[k]}\,|\,V\text{ cyclically row-semistrict}\,\}.

Elements of this set correspond bijectively to
tableaux on the right-hand side of (ii):
again modify
V
by marking entries belonging to
K
.
∎

Combining these bijections with Proposition
6.5
,
we obtain the following type-
\mathsf{BC}
analogs
of the results in
Proposition
9.1
.

Proposition 9.5
.

Fix
w\in\mathfrak{B}_{n}
avoiding the patterns
3412
and
4231
with
F_{w}\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{BC}}_{\mathrm{Z}}([\overline{n},n])

having path matrix
A
,
and define
A^{+}
,
A^{-}
as in (
6.11
).
Let
Q=Q(w)
be the type-
\mathsf{BC}
unit interval order defined before
Proposition
7.6
.
We have

\displaystyle\mathrm{per}(A^{+})

\displaystyle=\#\{U\in\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{BC}}(F_{w},n)\,|\,U\text{ left row-strict}\,\}

\displaystyle=\#\{U\in\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{BC}}(Q,n)\,|\,U\text{ descent-free}\,\}

\displaystyle=\#\{U\in\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{BC}}(Q,n)\,|\,U\text{ excedance-free}\,\}

\displaystyle=\#\text{ marked acyclic orientations of $\mathrm{inc}(Q)$},

\displaystyle\mathrm{per}(A^{-})

\displaystyle=\#\{U\in\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{BC}}(F_{w},n)\,|\,U\text{ left row-strict with no grounded paths}\,\}

\displaystyle=\#\{U\in\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{BC}}(Q,n)\,|\,U\text{ descent-free with no grounded elements}\,\}

\displaystyle=\#\{U\in\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{BC}}(Q,n)\,|\,U\text{ excedance-free with no grounded elements}\,\}

\displaystyle=\#\text{ acyclic orientations of $\mathrm{inc}(Q)$ with no grounded vertices},

\displaystyle\det(A^{+})

\displaystyle=\#\{U\in\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{BC}}(Q,1^{n})\,|\,U\text{ column-strict with at most $1$
grounded element}\,\}

\displaystyle=\#\text{ proper marked $\mathsf{BC}$-colorings of $\mathrm{inc}(Q)$ of type }(n,\emptyset),

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}2^{k}&\text{if $Q$ is a chain with $k\leq 1$ grounded elements},\\
0&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

\displaystyle\det(A^{-})

\displaystyle=\#\{U\in\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{BC}}(Q,1^{n})\,|\,U\text{ column-strict with no grounded elements}\,\}

\displaystyle=\#\text{ proper marked $\mathsf{BC}$-colorings of $\mathrm{inc}(Q)$ of type }(\emptyset,n),

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}1&\text{if $Q$ is a chain with no grounded elements},\\
0&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

\displaystyle\mathrm{Imm}_{\psi^{n}}^{\smash{\mathfrak{S}_{n}}}(A^{+})

\displaystyle=\#\{U\in\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{BC}}(F_{w},n)\,|\,U\text{ cylindrical}\,\},

\displaystyle=\#\{U\in\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{BC}}(Q,n)\,|\,U\text{ cyclically row-semistrict}\,\},

\displaystyle=\#\{U\in\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{BC}}(Q,n)\,|\,U\text{ record-free, row-semistrict}\,\},

\displaystyle=n\cdot\#\{U\in\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{BC}}(Q,n)\,|\,U\text{ right-anchored, row-semistrict}\,\},

\displaystyle=\#\text{ marked acyclic orientations of $\mathrm{inc}(Q)$ with one source},

\displaystyle\mathrm{Imm}_{\psi^{n}}^{\smash{\mathfrak{S}_{n}}}(A^{-})=\#\{U\in\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{BC}}(F_{w},n)\,|\,U\text{ cylindrical with no grounded paths}\,\},

\displaystyle=\#\{U\in\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{BC}}(Q,n)\,|\,U\text{ cyclically row-semistrict with no grounded elements}\,\},

\displaystyle=\#\{U\in\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{BC}}(Q,n)\,|\,U\text{ record-free, row-semistrict with no grounded elements}\,\},

\displaystyle=n\cdot\#\{U\in\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{BC}}(Q,n)\,|\,U\text{ right-anchored, row-semistrict with no grounded elements}\,\},

\displaystyle=\#\text{ acyclic orientations of $\mathrm{inc}(Q)$ with one source and
no grounded vertices}.

Proof.

By Proposition
6.5
,

\mathrm{per}(A^{+})
counts all families in
\Pi^{\mathsf{BC}}(F_{w})
,
equivalently (
9.10
) all left row-strict tableaux
in
\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{BC}}(F_{w},n)
. By Lemma
9.4
this equals
the number of descent-free marked
Q
-tableaux, and by
(
9.7
) it equals the number of
excedance-free marked
Q
-tableaux.
By the algorithms at the end of Subsection
8.1
,
this also equals the number of marked acyclic orientations of
\mathrm{inc}(Q)
.
By Proposition
6.5
,
\mathrm{per}(A^{-})
counts the same
assuming that
\ell_{t}(w)=0
, i.e., that

Q
has no grounded elements, and is
0
otherwise.

Now let

\sigma=(\sigma_{\overline{n}},\dotsc,\sigma_{\overline{1}},\sigma_{1},\dotsc,\sigma_{n})

be the unique path family in
\Pi^{\mathsf{BC}}_{e}(F_{w})
,
so that
Q
is the poset on
\sigma_{1},\dotsc,\sigma_{n}
,
with grounded elements
\sigma_{1},\dotsc,\sigma_{k}
for some
k
.
By Proposition
6.5
,

\det(A^{+})
counts the families
\pi\in\Pi^{\mathsf{BC}}(F_{w})

in which only
\pi_{\overline{1}}
and
\pi_{1}
may share a vertex.
This number is nonzero if and only if
Q
is an
n
-element chain.
If
\sigma_{\overline{1}}
and
\sigma_{1}
do not share a vertex, then
\sigma

is the unique such family and
\det(A^{+})=1
.
In this case,
Q
has no grounded element and
|\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{BC}}(Q,1^{n})|=1
.
If on the other hand
\sigma_{\overline{1}}
and
\sigma_{1}
do share a vertex,
then exactly one other path family
\pi\in\Pi^{\mathsf{BC}}(F_{w})

is counted and we have
\det(A^{+})=2
.
In this case,
Q
has one grounded element
and
|\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{BC}}(Q,1^{n})|=2
because the element may appear
with or without a star in a marked
Q
-tableau.
More simply,

\det(A^{-})
is
1
if
F_{w}=F_{e}
and is
0
otherwise.
Equivalently,
\det(A^{-})
is
1

if
Q
is a chain with no grounded element and
is
0
otherwise.

By Proposition
6.5
,
\mathrm{Imm}_{\psi^{n}}^{\smash{\mathfrak{S}_{n}}}(A^{+})
equals

n
times the number of path families
\pi

in
\Pi^{\mathsf{BC}}(F_{w})
such that

\varphi(\mathrm{type}(\pi))\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}
is an
n
-cycle.
This is the number of cylindrical tableaux in
\mathcal{U}^{\mathsf{BC}}(F_{w},n)

because each family
\pi
can be arranged in the orders

(\pi_{j},\pi_{v(j)},\pi_{v(v(j))},\dotsc,\pi_{v^{-1}(j)})

for
j=1,\dotsc,n
to create
n
cylindrical
\pi
-tableaux.
The remaining interpretations follow from
(
9.8
).

\mathrm{Imm}_{\psi^{n}}(A^{-})
is the same,
assuming that
\ell_{t}(w)=0
, i.e., that

Q
has no grounded elements, and is
0
otherwise.
∎

To combinatorially interpret evaluations of
\mathfrak{B}_{n}
-traces,
and state type-
\mathsf{BC}
analogs of the results in Theorem
9.2
,
we will use
pairs
of path-tableaux, poset-tableaux,
and acyclic orientations.
Define a
Young bidiagram of shape
(\lambda,\mu)
to be a
pair of Young diagrams of shapes
\lambda
and
\mu
.
Given a type-
\mathsf{BC}
path family

\pi=(\pi_{\overline{n}},\dotsc,\pi_{\overline{1}},\pi_{1},\dotsc,\pi_{n})

covering a zig-zag network
F_{w}\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{BC}}_{\mathrm{Z}}([\overline{n},n])
,
we fill Young bidiagram of shape
(\lambda,\mu)

with the paths

(\pi_{1},\dotsc,\pi_{n})
, keeping grounded paths
in the left diagram.
We call the resulting pair
(U,V)
of tableaux an
F_{w}
-bitableau
or
more specifically, a
\pi
-bitableau
of shape
(\lambda,\mu)
.
If
\pi
has type
v\in\mathfrak{B}_{n}
,
then we also say that each
\pi
-bitableau has
type
v
.
Let
\mathcal{B}(F_{w},\lambda,\mu)
be the set of all
F_{w}
-bitableaux
of shape
(\lambda,\mu)
.
If
(U,V)
is a
\pi
-bitableau of type
v
with
\varphi(v)=e
,
then
we may use
(
9.12
) to replace paths
\pi_{1},\dotsc,\pi_{n}
in
(U,V)

with the elements of
Q(w)
, marking each grounded element
i
in
U

with a star if
\mathrm{snk}(\pi_{i})<0
. We call the resulting structure a

marked
Q(w)
-tableau
.
Let
\mathcal{B}(Q(w),\lambda,\mu)
be the set of all marked
Q(w)
-bitableaux
of shape
(\lambda,\mu)
.

For example, consider
F_{2345\overline{1}}\in\mathcal{S}^{\mathsf{BC}}_{\mathrm{D}}([\overline{5},5])
and the
unique path families
\pi
of type
2\overline{1}345
and
\tau
of
type
21345
covering
F_{2345\overline{1}}
,

If
(U,V)
is an
F_{w}
-bitableau or
Q(w)
-bitableau,
then
U
,
V
could have some of the properties enumerated
between (
9.2
) and
Proposition
.
Some combinations of these
may be used to interpret trace evaluations as in
Theorems
9.6
–
9.9
.

Theorem 9.6
.

Let
w\in\mathfrak{B}_{n}
avoid the patterns
3412
and
4231
and let
Q=Q(w)
be the
related type-
\mathsf{BC}
unit interval order.
For each bipartition
(\lambda,\mu)\vdash n
we have

\displaystyle(\epsilon\epsilon)^{\lambda,\mu}(\widetilde{C}_{w}^{\mathsf{BC}}(1))

\displaystyle=\#\{(U,V)\in\mathcal{B}(Q,\lambda^{\negthickspace\top\negthickspace},\mu^{\negthickspace\top\negthickspace})\,|\,U,V\,\text{column-strict\,}\},

\displaystyle(\epsilon\eta)^{\lambda,\mu}(\widetilde{C}_{w}^{\mathsf{BC}}(1))

\displaystyle=\#\{(U,V)\in\mathcal{B}(Q,\lambda^{\negthickspace\top\negthickspace},\mu)\,|\,U\,\text{column-strict},V\,\text{row-semistrict\,}\},

\displaystyle(\eta\epsilon)^{\lambda,\mu}(\widetilde{C}_{w}^{\mathsf{BC}}(1))

\displaystyle=\#\{(U,V)\in\mathcal{B}(Q,\lambda,\mu^{\negthickspace\top\negthickspace})\,|\,U\,\text{row-semistrict},V\,\text{column-strict\,}\},

\displaystyle(\eta\eta)^{\lambda,\mu}(\widetilde{C}_{w}^{\mathsf{BC}}(1))

\displaystyle=\#\{(U,V)\in\mathcal{B}(Q,\lambda,\mu)\,|\,U,V\,\text{row-semistrict\,}\}.

Proof.

Let
F=F_{w}
have path matrix
A
,
fix
(\lambda,\mu)\vdash n
with
|\lambda|=k
,
and let
\theta=(\zeta\xi)^{\lambda,\mu}
be one of the characters
in the theorem.
By Lemma
6.3
and Theorem
6.4
, we have

(9.18)

(\zeta\xi)^{\lambda,\mu}(\widetilde{C}_{w}^{\mathsf{BC}}(1))=\mathrm{Imm}_{(\zeta\xi)^{\lambda,\mu}}^{\mathfrak{B}_{n}}(A)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subseteq[n]\\
|I|=k\end{subarray}}\mathrm{Imm}_{\zeta^{\lambda}}^{\smash{\mathfrak{S}_{k}}}(A^{+}_{I,I})\mathrm{Imm}_{\xi^{\mu}}^{\smash{\mathfrak{S}_{n-k}}}(A^{-}_{[n]\smallsetminus I,[n]\smallsetminus I}).

By (
6.4
), each of the type-
\mathsf{A}
immanants above is a
sum of products of determinants
or permanents
of matrices in

\{A^{+}_{J,J}\,|\,J\subset I\}

or
\{A^{-}_{J,J}\,|\,J\subset[n]\smallsetminus I\}
.
By Proposition
9.5
each
factor counts
certain one-row or one-column tableaux and their product counts
bitableaux of the required shape.
∎

Corollary 9.7
.

Let
w\in\mathfrak{B}_{n}
avoid the patterns
3412
and
4231
and have zig-zag network
F_{w}
and
type-
\mathsf{BC}
unit interval order
Q=Q(w)
.
In Theorem
9.6

we may replace
(marked) column-strict
Q
-tableaux of shape
\nu
with
(marked) colorings of
\mathrm{inc}(Q)
of type
\nu
.
We may also replace
(marked) row-semistrict
Q
-tableaux of shape
\nu

with either of the following:

(i)

(marked) excedance-free
Q
-tableaux of shape
\nu
,

(ii)

(marked) row-closed, left row-strict
F_{w}
-tableaux of shape
\nu
.

We also have that
(\eta\eta)^{\lambda,\mu}(\widetilde{C}_{w}^{\mathsf{BC}}(1))
equals

(iii)

the number of
marked acyclic orientations of subgraph sequences

(9.19)

(\mathrm{inc}(Q_{I_{1}}),\dotsc,\mathrm{inc}(Q_{I_{r}}),\mathrm{inc}(Q_{J_{1}}),\dotsc,\mathrm{inc}(Q_{J_{t}}))

where

(I_{1},\dotsc,I_{\ell(\lambda)},J_{1},\dotsc,J_{\ell(\mu)})

varies over all ordered set partitions of
[n]
of type

(\lambda_{1},\dotsc,\lambda_{\ell(\lambda)},\mu_{1},\dotsc,\mu_{\ell(\mu)}),

and all grounded vertices appear in
I_{1},\dotsc,I_{\ell(\lambda)}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
9.5
,
one-rowed tableaux
in Theorem
9.6

which are row-semistrict
(descent-free)
correspond bijectively to one-rowed tableaux
with properties

(i)
or
(ii)

and to acyclic orientations
(iii)

of
\mathrm{inc}(Q)
.
Thus there is a correspondence of
several-rowed tableaux and of acyclic orientations of subgraph sequences.
In all cases, we have equality of
the indices of paths, poset elements, and vertices which are grounded,
so markings correspond in the obvious way.
∎

For example, consider evaluating traces at
\widetilde{C}_{2345\overline{1}}^{\mathsf{BC}}(1)
by
applying Theorem
9.6

to the poset
Q(2345\overline{1})
in (
9.17
).
The first bitableau in (
9.17
)
contributes to

(\epsilon\eta)^{21,11}(\widetilde{C}_{2345\overline{1}}^{\mathsf{BC}}(1))
and

(\eta\eta)^{21,11}(\widetilde{C}_{2345\overline{1}}^{\mathsf{BC}}(1))
,
the second to
(\epsilon\epsilon)^{21,2}(\widetilde{C}_{2345\overline{1}}^{\mathsf{BC}}(1))
,

(\epsilon\eta)^{21,11}(\widetilde{C}_{2345\overline{1}}^{\mathsf{BC}}(1))
,

(\eta\epsilon)^{21,2}(\widetilde{C}_{2345\overline{1}}^{\mathsf{BC}}(1))
,

(\eta\eta)^{21,11}(\widetilde{C}_{2345\overline{1}}^{\mathsf{BC}}(1))
,
the third to
(\eta\eta)^{21,11}(\widetilde{C}_{2345\overline{1}}^{\mathsf{BC}}(1))
,
and the fourth to
(\eta\epsilon)^{21,2}(\widetilde{C}_{2345\overline{1}}^{\mathsf{BC}}(1))

and
(\eta\eta)^{21,11}(\widetilde{C}_{2345\overline{1}}^{\mathsf{BC}}(1))
.
If we modify the criteria of the theorem
as in Corollary
9.7
(i),
the same is true,
since a tableau with two or
fewer columns is
Q
-excedance-free if and only
if it is
Q
-row-semistrict.

If instead we modify the criteria of the theorem
as in Corollary
9.7
(ii) we may
apply these to path families
covering
F_{2345\overline{1}}
in (
9.15
).
In this case, we find that the third and fourth
tableaux in (
9.16
)
contribute to
(\eta\eta)^{21,11}(\widetilde{C}_{2345\overline{1}}^{\mathsf{BC}}(1))
,
and that the fourth tableau also contributes to

(\eta\epsilon)^{21,2}(\widetilde{C}_{2345\overline{1}}^{\mathsf{BC}}(1))
.
The tableaux contribute to no additional evaluations listed in
Theorem
9.6
because
the left tableau of the first bitableau is not left row-semistrict,
the left tableau of the second bitableau is not row-closed,
and the left tableaux of all four bitableaux fail to have the property
that source and sink indices are equal.

Finally, we may modify the criteria of the theorem
by applying Corollary
9.7
(iii) to

(9.20)
